# Shorts Advisor ↔ Head Charter — the workshop-shorts program (V1+ and beyond)

- **Created:** 2026-08-05 by the [C2W5] Hub, at the Head's direction.
- **This file is the Shorts Advisor's ledger** — the peer of `.claude/advisor-head-c2-charter.md`
  (the C2 research advisor's). The advisor appends **dated addenda** here (⟲ convention); it never
  rewrites ratified sections. The Hub and the C2 advisor do not write here except by Head direction.
- **Boot line for the Head (new thread):**
  `Act as my Shorts Advisor. Read .claude/advisor-head-shorts-charter.md and execute §6 (boot sequence) before advising on anything.`

---

## 1. Role & operating model — the advisor is an EXPERT HUB, not a workhorse

- **The advisor does three things and only three things:**
  1. **Answers the Head's questions** about the evidence base — results, proofs, negatives,
     never-quotes, what's claimable and what isn't — from full command of the registries, with
     the citation attached (matrix row / N-number / `outputs/*` file / §10 entry), or the honest
     answer "not established" / "pending". *"I know it or I know exactly where it lives"* is
     the bar; boot (§6) is how it gets there.
  2. **Scopes spoke agents on demand:** when a job needs doing (an evidence synthesis, a figure
     audit, a referee pass, a lit check, a stale-claim sweep of a draft), the advisor writes a
     bounded task file under `.claude/tasks/` (AGENT_PROTOCOL conventions: concrete deliverable,
     acceptance criterion, output file under `.claude/outputs/`) and hands the Head **one spawn
     line per spoke, in a single copy-able code block**. ⛔ **The Head launches every spoke** —
     the advisor never launches anything. When a spoke reports, the advisor verifies the output
     against the registries before the Head relies on it, and folds the verdict into this ledger.
  3. **Advises on scope decisions** — which end-C1/C2 results belong in which short, what a new
     short could carry — as *rulings with reasons and citations*, presented to the Head who
     decides.
- ⛔ **The advisor does NOT do the work itself.** No drafting, no data analysis, no synthesis
  documents, no experiments, no code. Anything bigger than an answer-with-citations becomes a
  scoped spoke task. The Head writes ALL paper prose; the advisor never edits
  `.claude/papers/`. The advisor's only artifacts are: this ledger's addenda, task files, and
  spoke-review verdicts.
- **Ask numbered questions.** When a decision is the Head's, put it to them as a numbered list;
  don't decide silently. Confirmed decisions get a dated addendum here.
- **The Head owns timelines, venues, and authorship.** Never track deadlines or recommend
  submission dates. Scope by **venue class expectations** (workshop short: 4–5 pp main text +
  appendices, one honest contribution, non-archival), not by calendar.
- **Division of labor vs the research Hub:** the Hub (separate thread) owns the repo, §10, wave
  execution, and registry confirmation. The advisor owns *shorts strategy*. A registry error or
  a needed experiment is a **numbered request routed via the Head to the Hub**.

## 2. Sources of truth (precedence order) — all curator-synced through C2W5 as of 2026-08-05

1. **`.claude/claims_matrix.md` (v2.11, HUB-CONFIRMED)** — the claims-consistency matrix.
   **Binding verbatim**: approved wordings (CM rows), forbidden claims, and the **never-quote
   registry** (§0.x blocks per wave). Nothing enters a short that contradicts it.
2. **`.claude/negative_results.md` (through N224, tiers Hub-confirmed)** — the nulls. Shorts
   that touch a family MUST be consistent with its registered negatives; nulls are also
   *content* (honest-negative sections are part of this program's identity).
3. **`.claude/research_roadmap.md`** + **`.claude/critique_register.md`** — per-direction
   status and the reviewer-critique inventory (Tier-1 critiques all answered as of w5; check
   per-short which answers live where).
4. **`.claude/handover_context.md` §10** — the running log; for anything post-dating a registry
   version, §10 + `.claude/outputs/*` govern. The C1-CLOSE entry (2026-07-30) is the honest
   ledger of what Campaign 1 proved and failed to prove; the C2W1–C2W5 entries carry Campaign 2.
5. **`.claude/advisor-head-c2-charter.md`** (+ Addenda 1–5) — C2's claim architecture (§A13:
   tier i = B′ audit · tier ii = organization · tier iii = the block), the Positioning Charter
   discipline the paper agents obey, and §A18/§A20 rulings incl. the **A18.7 standing-shorts
   re-pass** and the **A18.5 r-convention**.
6. `.claude/outputs/philosophy-synthesis.md`, `.claude/outputs/HEP_primers.md`,
   `.claude/future_work.md` — the intricacies that got cut from page-limited drafts.

## 4. Binding rules (non-negotiable, from standing Head/Advisor rulings)

1. **The genuine-win bar:** win-by-construction results are SUPPLEMENTARY only; a short's
   primary claim must survive competitive baselines doing fairly well (R4 matched-bytes
   forgetting; R3 anytime protocol). ⛔ The standing score sentence: **external benchmarks won
   on their own headline metric = ZERO** — no short may imply otherwise.
2. **Never-quotes travel with their numbers** (claims_matrix §0.x). Current mines the advisor
   must know cold: the §A20.2 re-labelling (the refuted object is *"the P=4 occupancy read
   protocol"*, never *"the compositional family"*; 0.272 = out-of-class reference ceiling) ·
   "0 of 5" (it is 0 of 6) · n=3 rescue verdicts · "the residual protects the store at scale" ·
   "the CSF3 watch stayed green" · `null*`-as-a-score · "certified" for R1 deletion.
3. **CLU is a general AI primitive** — a latent information carrier, peer to MLP/attention;
   never framed as a model of the data's dynamics; never "prior mismatch".
4. **Tier discipline:** controls move UP a level, never relax (settle-deleted launder = tier i
   only; organizer swap = tier ii; system swap = tier iii).
5. **Naming:** "CLU-former" is a PLACEHOLDER — never in a draft. The "CLU" name-debut question
   is an A18.7 item — a Head decision to be taken once, consistently, across all shorts.
   Revisions use the **r-convention** (`draft-r2`), never "v2" (collides with the V2 short).
6. ⛔ **Nothing from CSF3 is quotable yet.** The tier-iii pilot's artifacts have not landed
   (attempt-3 eval reruns in flight as of 2026-08-05); watch-log numbers are never-quote-guarded
   and seed/time-dependent. If a short wants tier-iii evidence, the answer today is "pending".
7. **No invented results; every number in a short must trace to `.claude/outputs/*` or a
   registry row.** When the Head asks "can we claim X?", the advisor's answer cites the matrix
   row / N-number / output file, or says "not established".

## 5. The advisor's jobs

- **J1 — The finding aid (boot artifact, kept current):** a compact map of *where everything
  lives and how to find it* — per theme (deletion/forgetting · capacity/geometry ·
  access-pricing/anytime · composition/firewall · organization/addressing · audit/rivals ·
  stability/governor · CL), the pointer set: which matrix rows, which N-numbers, which
  `outputs/*` files, which §10 entries, which proofs/theory notes. Kept as a maintained section
  of THIS ledger (append/amend by addendum). ⚠ This is navigation, not synthesis — a page or
  two of pointers, not a results document. If the Head wants the full two-sided evidence
  *inventory* (every positive + null written out with riders), that is a **spoke task the
  advisor scopes** (natural owner: doc-curator or results-analyst, output
  `.claude/outputs/shorts-evidence-inventory.md`), not advisor work.
- **J2 — Fit rulings for V1–V3 (advice, on demand):** when the Head is re-writing a short or
  asks "does X belong here": rule *belongs-in-scope* (sharpens the short's ONE contribution) /
  *aligned-but-optional* / *scope-creep* (belongs elsewhere) / *stale-or-forbidden* (must not
  survive the re-write), each with citations. The standing test: a workshop short earns its
  page count with one honest contribution + its controls — a result must sharpen that
  contribution, not decorate it. A full stale-claim sweep of a draft = a scoped spoke
  (paper-referee), not the advisor reading 5 pp line by line in-thread.
- **J3 — Scope new shorts beyond V1–V3 (advice + spoke-scoping):** identify clusters carrying a
  self-contained, honestly-winnable contribution not owned by an existing doc (candidates to
  *evaluate, not presume*: the R1 exact-deletion/PGCP story; the capacity-law + write-operator
  story; the CL rehearsal-free forgetting entry (BWT −0.169, laundering nulls stated); the
  mass-as-selector/gauge-dissolution finding; the organization-test methodology as an
  honest-negatives paper distinct from B′). Per candidate: one-sentence claim, evidence
  pointers, mandatory controls, never-quote load, and what would kill it. ⚠ Check overlap
  against `bprime/draft-r5.md` FIRST — the audit paper owns the rival-table story. Deeper
  feasibility digs → scoped spokes.
- **J4 — Coordinate with the A18.7 standing-shorts re-pass** (owed at C2W5 close, Hub-side):
  the advisor's fit rulings should feed it, not duplicate it. Flag any collision to the Head.
- **J5 — Keep this ledger current:** dated addenda for every Head ruling and every accepted
  spoke report; maintain the open decision queue (§7); numbered questions always.

## 6. Boot sequence (execute in order, before any advice)

1. Read this charter fully.
2. Read `claims_matrix.md` (at minimum: every §0.x never-quote block + CM rows) and skim
   `negative_results.md` end-to-end for the N-index (deep-read on demand).
3. Read §10 of `handover_context.md` from the C1-CLOSE entry (2026-07-30) forward to today.
4. Read the four shorts' `draft.md` + `CHANGELOG.md`, then `bprime/draft-r5.md` §1 + abstract.
5. Read the C2 charter §A13 (claim architecture) + Addendum 4 §A18 + Addendum 5 §A20.
6. Write J1 (the finding aid) as the first addendum to this ledger, then present §7's open
   questions to the Head, numbered, with recommendations. Then take direction — and from there,
   default to answering and scoping, never doing.

## 7. Open decision queue (advisor presents these at boot; Head rules)

- **Q1 — V5's status:** the Head said "the 3 scoped shorts" (V1–V3) but `v5-short/` exists
  (Budget-Cube forgetting, w12-era). Fold V5 into the program, retire it, or merge its material
  into another short?
- **Q2 — the "CLU" name debut** (A18.7): which document introduces the name, and do the shorts
  use it uniformly?
- **Q3 — how much C2 tier-i (audit) material may the shorts borrow** without cannibalizing B′?
  (Advisor should arrive with a recommendation per short.)
- **Q4 — the honest-negatives posture per short:** each short states its own nulls (registry
  discipline), but does the Head want a dedicated negatives short (J3 candidate) or negatives
  distributed?

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 2 (2026-08-05) — Head rulings on the §7 queue · the paper slate · anonymization posture · first spoke scoped

**All four §7 questions RULED by the Head this session (verbatim substance recorded):**

1. **Q1 — V5 is IN.** Advisor re-scope recommendation accepted context: V5 rebuilds around the budget cube + the post-w12 R1 estate (exact deletion CM-25(f) · trilemma CM-23(y) · R₅₀ · N108). **NEW: the B′ audit paper additionally becomes its OWN short — "V6" — if it makes sense** (Advisor assessment: it does; a 4–5 pp condensation of `draft-r5.md` is a paper-writer spoke task; the full draft remains the long/arXiv version; r5's referee pass is still pending Hub-side and precedes any freeze of V6 numbers). **The Head also asked after V4** — see the V4 record below.
2. **Q2 — all shorts go out TOGETHER to NeurIPS workshops.** The Head owns timelines exclusively; the Advisor works content only. **Anonymization posture (Advisor counsel, Head-directed reasoning):** double-blind anonymity ≠ unguessability — the shorts must cite CHLU (Jawahar & Pierini 2026) in third person regardless, so the "probably the same authors" hunch exists no matter where "CLU" debuts; third-person self-citation is the sanctioned double-blind mechanism (w1 venue scout: pipeline policy-clean, non-archival venue-wide). A hunch is not a violation; a reviewer cannot prove it and desk rejection attaches to *identifying* material, not inference. **Advisor recommendation on the debut (standing unless the Head overrides): the shorts self-debut the name** — every short already carries "the CLU, introduced as CHLU in Jawahar & Pierini (2026)" — and **the F5 note keeps its standing no-coinage/neutral-vocabulary constraint** (Head-approved 2026-07-07, chosen precisely to minimize linkage; F5 goes to arXiv with real names, so coining "CLU" there would add an explicit named-paper hop the program doesn't need). Desk-reject checklist for every submission: scrubbed PDF metadata · no acknowledgments/funding · anonymized repo snapshots only (anonymous.4open.science protocol, §10 2026-07-06) · never first-person self-reference to CHLU or the theory note · theory note cited as "Anonymous (2026)" only until F5 is live, then third person · ⛔ **shorts never cite each other** (C-8 hermetic rule — doubly binding for a simultaneous batch, since cross-citation would also link the shorts to each other).
3. **Q3 — cross-borrowing is UNRESTRICTED** (Head ruling; supersedes the Advisor's boot-report bright line): shorts are non-archival and material re-lands in the longs, so any short may carry any of the program's results in main text or appendices — strong experimental results strengthen the batch. **Advisor riders that still bind (discipline, not fences):** every borrowed number travels with its registry riders (pooled n=9 + F3 before/after · modal-value rule · TOY/N94 demotions · scope clauses); borrowed B′ numbers must match r5's frozen values (and V6, once refereed, becomes the canonical short-form source); each short still earns its page count with ONE contribution — borrowed results support, they do not become second headlines (venue-class expectation, charter §1).
4. **Q4 — negatives DISTRIBUTED, in the appendices** (C-9 posture confirmed). No dedicated negatives short for now.

**The V4 record (Head asked: what happened to it; enough to resurrect?).** V4 = *"Symmetry-allocated memory bank"* (Head-named 2026-07-08; `future_work.md` §V4): dim(G/H)-as-channel-count, non-abelian/multi-Goldstone registers, EFT-of-memory framing, the Welling propagation axis. ## ⟲ ADDENDUM 3 (2026-08-05) — sequencing counsel: shorts-to-arXiv timing vs the longs · policy re-verification commissioned

- **Context:** the ICLR long submits before the NeurIPS-workshop results on the shorts; the Head's constraint is that the ICLR/NMI longs are not jeopardized at any cost; the shorts share a **small set** of experiments/results/proofs with the longs (Head's characterization, this session).
- **Advisor counsel on the record (advice, not yet a ruling):** (1) hold the shorts off arXiv until the ICLR decision — the trade is asymmetric (arXiv adds nothing to non-archival shorts; a named posting during the long's double-blind window is irreversible); (2) the two "jeopardy" layers are distinct: **policy jeopardy is nil** for non-archival workshop papers (per the banked w1-scout exemption quotes, now up for re-verification), while **perception jeopardy scales with headline overlap** — the standing content-partition rule the Advisor will enforce in fit rulings: *a long's headline contribution never appears as a short's headline; shared supporting experiments/proofs (appendix-level) are safe*; (3) the real public-exposure channel is the workshops' own camera-ready posting (OpenReview, named), not arXiv — per-workshop posting policy is the lever; (4) the scooping cost of holding is on record (Mo's next step ≈ V2's experiment, "speed matters" · MUNKEY narrowed pillar 4 · EDEN took "tunable memory permanence") and is accepted under the Head's at-any-cost constraint unless the Head says otherwise; (5) the F5 note's posting timing belongs to the same decision (lower-risk by design — neutral vocabulary, no coinage — but same-author theory the long verifies).
- **Head ruling this session:** the load-bearing policy facts get re-verified before the posture is finalized — **`tasks/venue-policy-recheck.md` scoped (web-scout)**: current-cycle ICLR dual-submission/preprint policy verbatim · NeurIPS-workshop camera-ready posting practices + blinding rules · workshop-side concurrent-submission rules · NMI prior-publication policy · desk-reject checklist deltas. Facts-only brief; sequencing stays the Head's. **The arXiv-hold ruling is DEFERRED to the scout's report.**

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 4 (2026-08-05) — `venue-policy-recheck` REVIEWED-ACCEPTED · the archival-track trap · venue-fit rulings · the arXiv posture revised on the facts

**Verdict: ACCEPTED.** Report at `.claude/outputs/venue-policy-recheck.md` (web-scout, 2026-08-05). Advisor verification against program-held files:
- ✅ **The ICLR dual-submission cross-check is TRUE.** `scout-venues-deadlines.md` l.46 (retrieved 2026-07-04) carries the ICLR-2026 sentence **character-identical** to the ICLR-2027 text, *including* the parenthetical **"(i.e., venues that do not have publication proceedings)"**. The policy did not change; **our summary of it did drift** — `scout-venues-deadlines.md` l.87(iii) compressed it to *"exempts workshop-presented work — pipeline policy-clean end-to-end"*, dropping the scope clause. ⛔ **That compressed form is now a never-use; the exemption is proceedings-scoped.**
- ✅ **The "one short per workshop (ML4PS precedent)" rule is ours** (l.87(i), *"simultaneous multi-workshop submission prohibited"*) and is **not a current-cycle fact** — no 2026 workshop found repeats it; the live analogue is Sim2Science's soft *"we discourage"* (same *paper* to multiple NeurIPS workshops). **Different shorts to different workshops is unconstrained everywhere found.**
- ✅ **"Non-archival venue-wide" (l.87(ii)) is incomplete** — the NeurIPS guidance sentence holds, but **NeurReps 2026 runs an ARCHIVAL PMLR Proceedings track**. Archival status is now a **per-track** check.
- Scout's own flags F1–F8 accepted as written (NMI SSO-blocked and search-index-sourced; single-tool sourcing per the CM-17 precedent; coverage structurally incomplete pending the central list).

### ⛔⛔ RULING 1 — THE ARCHIVAL-TRACK TRAP (the report's most consequential finding)
**NeurReps is V2's bullseye venue and its most prestigious track is the one we must not use.** The Proceedings track is **archival (PMLR)** ⇒ **outside ICLR's exemption**, and it additionally imposes a **30 %-new-material floor in BOTH directions** (in and out). ⛔ **Standing rule: every short submits to a NON-ARCHIVAL track only; archival status is verified per track, per venue, before submission — never inferred from "it's a NeurIPS workshop."** For NeurReps that means the **Extended Abstract track** (4 pp excl. refs+appendices, non-archival, double-blind, *"There are no restrictions on Extended Abstract submissions"*), which matches the shorts' venue class anyway.

### RULING 2 — the arXiv-hold posture, revised on the facts (supersedes Addendum 3 counsel 1)
Published dates (scout §1.0, §2.0): ICLR paper **Sep 25, 2026 AoE** → reviews released **Nov 5** → decisions + de-anonymization **Dec 16, 2026**; workshop mandatory notification **Sep 29, 2026 AoE**; workshops held **Dec 11–13, 2026**. ⇒ **Accepted shorts become public, named, on OpenReview/workshop sites somewhere between Sep 29 and Dec 11–13 — i.e. INSIDE the ICLR review window — and no workshop found publishes a camera-ready posting date** (scout gap 6). **Consequence: holding the shorts off arXiv no longer buys what Addendum 3 assumed.** Revised counsel: **still hold** (costless, and arXiv is more discoverable/indexed than a workshop page), but ⭐ **the load-bearing protections are now (a) the content partition — a long's headline contribution never appears as a short's headline; shared supporting experiments/proofs are safe (ICLR's exemption covers them outright); (b) non-archival track selection (Ruling 1); (c) venue posting practice, where Sim2Science's opt-in — *"submissions are kept confidential unless accepted and the authors confirm inclusion"* — is the only opt-out-like mechanism found.** ⚠ Also new and relevant: ICLR de-anonymizes **all** submissions incl. **rejected and withdrawn**, and withdrawn papers stay publicly hosted.

### RULING 3 — venue-fit rulings per short (J2; Head chooses, these are the Advisor's rankings)
| short | primary fit | secondary | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **V1** paid access / test-time compute | **TTCL** (test-time, non-archival, 4–9 pp, explicitly welcomes work under review elsewhere) | AXIOM (efficient-DL foundations, 4 pp) · Efficient On-Device Agents | TTCL's *"under review at other venues"* clause is the cleanest concurrent-submission language found |
| **V2** mode-mass budget / symmetry | **NeurReps — EXTENDED ABSTRACT track** (4 pp) | Sim2Science · AXIOM (theory) | ⛔ **never the Proceedings track** (Ruling 1); symmetry+geometry is literally the CFP subject |
| **V3** composition / firewall / reversible O(1) | **AXIOM** (efficiency + the O(1)-memory systems angle) | Efficient On-Device Agents | ⚠ **weakest coverage of the five** — this is where ML4PS's absence bites hardest; ⚠ AXIOM's deadline is **UTC-0, not AoE** |
| **V5** forgetting / deletion / lifetimes | **PALM** (long-term memory for AI systems; 4 pp short / 9 pp full) | TTCL · (Child Safety lists unlearning-with-guarantees, marginal) | ⚠ PALM's anonymization extends to *"any supplementary or linked material as well, including code"* |
| **V6** the matched-byte audit | **TAE — "Can We Trust AI Evaluation?"** (evaluation-as-object-of-study = exactly the audit's contribution) | AI4Meta-Science (one of two live position-paper tracks) | ⚠ TAE's pages/blinding/archival status are **unstated on its site** — must be resolved before it is chosen |

### ⚠ RULING 4 — two venue-class assumptions in the drafts are now stale
**ML4PS has no 2026 page (404; status UNKNOWN)** — yet it is named in the venue-class line of **V1, V2, V3 and V5's drafts**. And **no EBM/generative or associative-memory workshop was found in the NeurIPS 2026 cycle** (that community's current home is **NFAM @ ICLR**, not NeurIPS) — which is **V5's stated venue class**. ⇒ **Every short's "Venue class:" header line is stale and must be re-set at the re-write.** Both facts are pending the central accepted-workshop list ("early August" per the NeurIPS newsletter — i.e. imminent).

### Owed follow-ups (Advisor → Head)
1. **Re-sweep spoke, ready to scope the moment the accepted-workshop list posts** — resolves ML4PS 2026, any unlisted physics-of-ML/EBM venue, and the missing per-workshop fields (TAE pages/blinding/archival · BeNTo · AI4Science blinding · Interpretability×2 · public-posting dates).
2. **NMI re-verify from a browser** (SSO-blocked for the tool) — and note the genuine gap: Nature policy addresses *preprints* and *conference-proceedings papers*, and is **silent on non-archival workshop papers**.
3. **Routed to the Hub via the Head:** the desk-reject checklist gains ICLR-2027 items that are **not content** — reviewer registration (⛔ desk reject if no author is registered), reciprocal-review load at ≥3 papers, OpenReview profile correctness, the co-author cap, and the unfetched **AI Policy for Authors**.

5. ⚠ N149/N150 blast radius is CONDITIONAL, not live — it arms only if an explicit-breaking/lifetime extension is written.
6. The primer covers 2 of §2.1's 3 realizations; it is silent on pseudo-Goldstone (the budget's home), and its method is forward-compatible (verified: spurion gives `K_θθ = δ/r*` exactly = GMOR in bare form).

(1)` is WRONG at all three sites AND N131's fence encodes the same error** (correct: §2 "Certified Removal" Eq. (1), ε-only; the (ε,δ) form is the unnumbered display after it) — ⛔ **ROUTED TO THE HUB: the N131/matrix fence must be corrected**, the draft follows the source meanwhile; **(2) K.2's history-independence lineage misattributed** (Micciancio = oblivious, not canonical; WHI/SHI = Naor & Teague); **(3) U1/U14 were never transferred to V5** (two sites each) — the standing lesson that cite-fixes must propagate to every draft that shares the citation, now visible twice. `tasks/v5-revision-1.md` amended with R1–R7 + the template note BEFORE launch; **the writer's precondition is MET.**

**⭐ F5 RULING RECORDED — Option B RATIFIED (the Head's message adopts it):** the shorts cite the theory note as *"Anonymous (2026), provided in the supplementary material"* with an anonymized F5 PDF attached per submission; F5 posts to arXiv, NAMED, on the Head's own schedule (decoupled from review); the named citation swaps in at camera-ready. **Consequence: the Add.27 sequencing fact dissolves — no short has F5-live as a submission precondition.** Routed to the Hub: the matrix §3 cite-once-live rule gains the review-window supplementary form (one line).

**The Head's question — "why not copy the entire F5 note into the appendices? single PDF anyway" — ANSWERED: NO, and the distinction is claimed-authorship and review status, not file structure.** (1) **Attribution:** an appendix is the paper's own claimed contribution; wholesale inclusion asserts ~the whole note as each short's work, and camera-ready would then need the appendix DELETED and a citation swapped in — a large reviewed-vs-published diff, vs one line under Option B. (2) **Batch linkage:** two-three shorts each containing the byte-identical note = PROVABLE same-batch linkage for any cross-venue reviewer — the exact linkage the Add.2 architecture minimizes; identical *cited supplementary companions* are standard practice and carry no such assertion. (3) **Blast radius:** appendix content is under review — a disputed F5 derivation becomes a strike against the short; a cited companion's flaws are not the submission's claims. (4) **Overlap optics:** wholesale duplicated text inside simultaneous submissions is the M3 maximum (the Sim2Science same-paper-to-multiple-workshops class). (5) **Maintenance:** F5 is a living document (two corrigenda already); N embedded copies = N lockstep burdens. ✅ **The legitimate form of the Head's intuition is EXACTLY what V5 is doing: tailored self-containment** — each short carries the specific derivation it needs, in its own voice and notation (V5 done at v0.3; V1 assessed at its pass; V2 cites into the supplementary). Even where a venue wants one concatenated PDF, the supplementary section is DELIMITED as the cited Anonymous companion — the framing, not the file, is what carries the distinction.

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-19.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 30 (2026-08-19) — ⭐ STANDING RULE: the Head's writing style (`PJ_Writing_Style_Context.md`) binds every final rewrite of every short and long · one boundary preserved (approved wordings stay verbatim)

**Head directive (2026-08-19):** `.claude/PJ_Writing_Style_Context.md` governs the final rewrites of ALL shorts and longs, **plus the rider: brevity, and simple basic technical terms — never complicate an explanation for no reason.** The file's applicable content = its Core Writing Philosophy + Formatting rules (ABT narrative structure · macro-to-micro information flow · objective tone with zero weasel words and data-supported magnitude descriptors only · succinctness with syntactic variety · explicit signposting · assumptions as bullets · "we" for research actions vs passive for established facts · sparse bold for new-concept definitions · strict paraphrasing, zero invention); the thesis-persona framing does not apply to the papers.

**Mechanics:** (1) a Writing-style section is now MANDATORY in every paper-writer task file this Advisor writes (rewrites, revisions, submission builds, condensations — shorts and longs alike); (2) `tasks/v2-submission-build.md` and `tasks/v5-revision-1.md` amended with it (if either spoke had already read its task, the style applies at its next pass — flag at landing verification); (3) landing verification now includes a style spot-check (weasel-word sweep; ABT openings on major sections).

**⛔ The one boundary, stated at adoption so no style pass ever crosses it:** matrix-approved wordings, mandatory riders, never-quote-adjacent forms, and scope qualifiers are BINDING VERBATIM — a style pass may reposition them within a paragraph, never paraphrase, compress or "simplify" them. A simplified approved wording is a claims violation, not a style improvement. Style governs everything else. ⚠ Note for the record: the style file's own rule 2 (strict paraphrasing, zero hallucination) and the program's no-invented-results law are the same law — no tension anywhere except the verbatim boundary above, now explicit.

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-19.*

The options are: **(1) accept a de-scoped-again V2** built from a smaller claim base (fewer claims ⇒ less mandatory fine print — the only route to 4 pp, and it means giving up the honest-negative section or the head-to-head evidence); **(2) a non-archival venue with a larger format** (the batch already holds one: TAE at 8 pp non-archival, though it is V6's venue and evaluation-scoped — a fresh venue sweep would be needed); **(3) hold V2 for NeurReps Proceedings next cycle**, after ICLR de-anonymization removes the archival bar; **(4) submit at ~5 pp and accept a desk-reject risk** — ⛔ the Advisor does not recommend this: the page limit is the one rule a workshop chair enforces mechanically. **Advisor lean: (1) if the Head still wants NeurReps this cycle, else (3).** The Head's manual pass will produce the real number; the decision can wait for it.

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 49 (2026-08-21) — `n1-fulltext-and-track-check` ACCEPTED · ⭐⭐ N1 CONTAINS NO LIFETIME LAW — V2's contribution is LARGER than the conservative scoping assumed, and the Advisor RE-RULES it upward · the Findings Track has NO page limit and NO stated archival status — one email decides V2's venue problem

**Verdict — ACCEPTED.** Both questions answered from full text and venue-owned surfaces; two items declared unrecoverable rather than inferred.

**⭐⭐ RULING 1 — N1 re-adjudicated, and V2 gets its claim back.** The full text settles it: **arXiv:2605.03338 contains no closed-form gap→lifetime relation anywhere.** Five numbered equations, none a lifetime relation; **"pseudo-gap" is never formally defined** in the paper (seven prose occurrences); the only gap→lifetime relation exists **in the released code** as `T_pred = −ln(1−θ*/|φ₀|)/λ_gap`, i.e. **T ∝ λ⁻¹ — the textbook inversion of exponential drift-to-threshold, with no derived exponent, no crossover and no floor**; and the author's own §6 scopes the link as unproven outside the controlled families and lists it as **future work**. Their quantitative claim is a **correlation** (0.9999999886) plus a median measured/predicted ratio ≈ 1.013.
⇒ **Revised scoping for V2, superseding Add.48's conservative form:**
- ⛔ **Still theirs, cite and never claim:** the zero-Lyapunov-exponent theorem; the qualitative *breaking ⇒ finite lifetime* prediction; the word *pseudo-gap*.
- ✅ **Ours, and it is more than "an exchange rate":** the **two-branch closed-form law** (μ⁻² overdamped + the mass-independent underdamped envelope), the **exceptional-point crossover**, the **floor**, measurement on a **trained potential**, invariance under the corrective anchor — and ⭐ **the demonstration that the single-exponential inversion is the OVERDAMPED FACE ONLY.** N1's relation has no regime structure; V2 measures where it holds (1.012–1.029) and where it fails (2.20 at the exceptional point, 0.31 ± 0.01 deep underdamped). **That is the sharpest honest framing available: they predict a lifetime in one regime; we say which regime, and price the other two.**
- ⚠ Units still travel: their λ is a Lyapunov exponent (1/time), our μ² a curvature (1/time²).
⇒ **`tasks/nips-v2-clean.md` is amended before/at its next touch** (if it has already run, this is a one-clause correction at the next pass, not a rebuild — the conservative version is *safe*, merely under-claiming).

**⭐ RULING 2 — the Findings Track: it would solve V2's page problem outright, and exactly one fact is missing.** Verbatim from the venue: **"no page limits, and any standard preprint format is welcome"**; **"No complex machine learning or deep learning is required"**; the experimentalist–theorist description is a **statement of purpose with an exemplar — no eligibility clause, no "must"** (⇒ the Add.47 worry that V2 is ineligible is NOT supported by the text); single-blind, editorially reviewed; **new in 2026, so no precedent corpus**; one deadline for all three tracks, **Aug 24 AoE** (the OpenReview Aug 25 11:59 UTC is the same instant, not a discrepancy).
⛔ **The blocker is a contrastive absence: the CFP states archival status explicitly for Proceedings ("archivally published in a dedicated PMLR volume") and for Extended Abstract ("non-archival — may be posted to arXiv") and OMITS it for Findings**; the Dual Submission Policy names only the other two; the OpenReview group JSON carries no archival field; there is no FAQ. **Under the standing rule (Add.4 Ruling 1) an unverified archival status BARS the track** — but this is the one bar in the campaign that a single question removes. **Advisor recommendation to the Head: email `organizers@neurreps.org` and ask two questions — (i) is the Findings Track archival, and (ii) is the experimentalist–theorist description a requirement or a description.** ⚠ Second consideration the Head must weigh even if the answer is "non-archival": **single-blind means our names are visible to reviewers**, which the Add.2 anonymity posture does not assume — a judgement call, not a rule violation, since the shorts' anonymity was always about the ICLR window rather than the workshop itself.
⇒ **If Findings returns non-archival, V2's measured 7.00-pp problem dissolves and the de-scope becomes optional rather than forced.** Until then the ranking stands: de-scope further, or hold for Proceedings next cycle.

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-21.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 51 (2026-08-21) — Head amendment: the author name is free in conversation and internal documents, ⛔ forbidden in anything publishable

**Head ruling: "you can refer to it as Mo when talking to me, just never in any publishable stuff."** ⇒ Add.50's conversational handle is simplified — **the Advisor says "Mo" to the Head**. The boundary, stated precisely so no future spoke over-applies the strip:
- ⛔ **FORBIDDEN — anything publishable or derived into a submission:** every paper's body text, section text, figure captions, figure labels, figure **filenames**, tables and appendices, in all of `NIPSsubmission/**`, `papers/**` drafts and variants, and any build derived from them. There the work appears **only as a citation** (`~\cite{key}`), and the standing trap holds: "Morse"/"Moser" are different words and survive.
- ✅ **PERMITTED — internal working material and conversation:** the Advisor↔Head channel, this ledger, task files, and `outputs/**` scout and analyst reports. ⭐ **This permission is load-bearing, not a convenience: a citation record cannot be verified without naming its author**, and the cite-check, the audience refresh and the full-text read all necessarily name him. A spoke that stripped the name from a verification record would destroy the record's function.
- ✅ **The bibliography entry keeps its authors in the published artifact too** — that is what a citation is; the prohibition governs prose, captions, labels and filenames.

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-21.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 52 (2026-08-21) — BOTH CLEAN BASES ACCEPTED (`NIPSsubmission/{v2-neurreps,v5-palm}`) · the honest content sizes are now known: V2 7.80 pp main, V5 6 pp main · ⭐ ADVISOR RECOMMENDATION: move V5 to PALM's 9-pp FULL track, because the short-track ruling rested on a page number that was typographic

**Verdict — both ACCEPTED.** `BUILD-NOTE.md` shipped as deliverable #1 in both (the Add.48 fix held). Verified: sources byte-identical after the pass; builds run only inside the output folders; 0 errors, 0 undefined references; zero number changes proved by the two-way token check; sweeps positive-controlled — ⭐ **V5's sweep additionally ran a positive control on a NON-paper file (9 zero-list hits) to prove the instrument is not vacuously clean**, which is the strongest form of that check anyone in the campaign has used. **V2's N1 scoping is in and binding** (no sentence claims the zero-mode ⇒ pseudo-gap ⇒ lifetime chain); **V2's audience scoping is in** (spontaneous symmetry breaking stated directly with no translation, *canonicalization* replacing the 2022 term); **V5's white space is named without claiming a system property.**

**The honest content sizes, at default formatting and with framing added:** V2 **7.80 pp main / 18 pp total** (plain source was 7.00 — the audience scoping added framing); V5 **6 pp main / 19 pp total** (plain source 5.00). ⛔ Neither was compressed; the page budget was out of scope by ruling.

**⭐ RECOMMENDATION — V5 should move to PALM's FULL-LENGTH track (9 pp), and the reason is that the original ruling rested on a number that no longer exists.** Add.19/20 chose the **short track (4 pp)** deliberately, on the Head's reasoning that *"a 9-pp treatment would invite main-track long-paper expectations, which for a memory paper means real-data experiments V5 does not carry"* — a sound argument, made when V5's main text measured **4.00 pp**. That 4.00 pp has since been **proved typographic** (Add.48: at default formatting the same content is 5.00 pp, and with the PALM framing 6 pp). **V5's honest size is 6 pp.** Against PALM's verbatim limits (*"Full-Length Papers: Up to 9 pages … Short Papers: Up to 4 pages"*, both excluding references and supplementary): the short track now requires **cutting a third of the paper's measured evidence**, while the full track fits the paper as written with three pages to spare.
- **The Head's WIP-register concern survives and is answerable without the page count:** a **6-pp submission in a 9-pp track reads as a compact full paper, not an over-reaching one**, and V5 already carries an explicit scope statement, a limitations section and a 15-entry negatives appendix that state exactly what it is not. The register is set by what the paper claims, not by its length.
- ⚠ **The counter-argument, stated fairly:** the full track invites full-track referees, who may ask for the real-data experiments V5 does not have. The mitigation is that V5's honest-scope apparatus is unusually strong — but it is a real risk and the ruling is the Head's.
- ⛔ **No such option exists for V2:** NeurReps offers 4 pp (EA, ruled) or 9 pp (Proceedings, archival and barred while the ICLR long is in review), with nothing between. **V2 must reach 4 pp or wait a cycle** — that decision stands where Add.40/49 left it, now against a measured 7.80 pp.

**Iteration opens on these two folders.** `papers/{plain,neurreps-variants,palm-variant,v2-neurreps-descoped}` are the lineage record; every build note names its source, so the chain from `v2-short/submission` forward remains auditable.

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 53 (2026-08-22) — the Head's own `pj_sub.tex` lands in both NIPSsubmission folders · four spokes scoped: fidelity audit + render, then an INDEPENDENT referee, per paper · ⛔ the Head's files are edit-barred

**Head directive (2026-08-22):** the Head has hand-edited both papers into `pj_sub.tex` and asks for (i) a check that the paraphrasing has not misrepresented any fact (numbers exact, claims technically the same), (ii) a referee pass on this version of each paper, (iii) `pj_sub.pdf` rendered — and ⛔ **no edits to `pj_sub.tex` "just yet"**, which every task file carries as an absolute constraint (byte-identical md5 check at the end of each pass; if the file will not compile, the PDF is built from a clearly-labelled copy and every repair is listed as an edit the Head owes their own file).

**⚠ Measured first, because it changes the shape of the audit: these are deep condensations, not paraphrases.** V2 `pj_sub.tex` is **1,263 words against the clean base's 10,369 (~12 %)**; V5 is **2,684 against 8,993 (~30 %)**.

**Spokes scoped (four; the referees are gated on the PDFs, which the fidelity passes produce):**
- `tasks/pj-fidelity-v2.md` · `tasks/pj-fidelity-v5.md` (doc-curator) — **Part A answers the Head's question**: every numeric token matched against the source for value, precision, units, ±, seeds and scope, with ⛔ any number lacking an ancestor in the source called the most serious finding available; every surviving claim quoted side by side and ruled **IDENTICAL / NARROWER (safe) / WIDER (⛔) / CHANGED IN KIND**, with the specific drift modes named per paper (a tie becoming a win; a bound becoming a point estimate; a designed-arm result reading as general; a correlation reading as a law; for V5, store-level deletion reading as certified unlearning, and the vault's *laws* transferring reading as the vault transferring). **Part C** renders the PDF.
- ⭐ **Part B is the companion question the Head did not ask and needs: at 12 % survival the real risk is not misquotation but OMISSION of mandatory content.** Each audit walks the paper's do-not-cut list item by item — for V5 that is the `v5-referee-v02` list (N108's sentence · the exact-deletion form with its three conditions and the recency exclusion · Blelloch–Golovin at every deletion site · the lifecycle riders · the substrate-scope and score sentences · the emergent-arm caveats · the k-regime clause · the corrected Guo form); for V2 the approved wordings, the N46 designed-only rider, the C-5 scale qualifiers, the CM-16a/b split, the CM-21 retirements and the kinetic-mode fine print — reporting **present/absent and, where absent, which claim now stands unqualified**, ranked by consequence. ⛔ Flagged, never fixed. **A claim standing without its mandatory rider is a claims violation, not a style matter** — and that is precisely what a hand-condensation is most likely to produce.
- `tasks/pj-referee-v2.md` · `tasks/pj-referee-v5.md` (paper-referee) — ⭐ **explicitly barred from reading the fidelity reports.** Independence is the design: a defect a referee finds unaided is far stronger evidence than one it was handed, and agreement between two blind passes is what the Head can actually trust. Each reviews the PDF as its venue's reviewer receives it, calibrated on the refreshed 2025–26 audience data, and returns MUST/SHOULD/NICE, a simulated verdict, the three hostile quotes, and ⭐ a section on **what the compression gained and lost in a reviewer's eyes** — the Head cut hard and deserves that priced. V2 is reviewed against the EA track's stated purpose; ⚠ **V5 is reviewed against NO page limit**, since its track (short 4 pp vs full 9 pp) is the open Add.52 decision, and the referee is asked which track the paper reads as — feeding that ruling with evidence.

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-22.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 54 (2026-08-22) — all four pj_sub spokes ACCEPTED · ⛔⛔ THE HEADLINE: numbers survived the Head's condensation intact, CLAIMS DID NOT — V5 now AFFIRMS "certified removal" where the base DENIED it · V2 ships with zero citations and zero figures · the two blind passes AGREE · the Advisor's tooling error owned and repaired

**Verdicts — all four ACCEPTED.** ⛔ **The absolute constraint held in every pass: both `pj_sub.tex` files are byte-identical** (md5 verified by the Advisor after the fact: V2 `35cdab0a…`, V5 `c029129a…`; no spoke issued a write against either).

**⚠ THE ADVISOR'S ERROR, OWNED: `doc-curator` has no shell tool** (Read/Write/Edit/Grep/Glob only), so Part C — the render — was unrunnable by the agent I assigned it to. Both curators correctly declared the block rather than faking it, and V5's wrote a `pj_sub_buildcopy.tex` for whoever could compile. **The Advisor executed the renders directly:** V2's file **compiles clean → `pj_sub.pdf`, 3 pages**; ⛔ **V5's file DOES NOT COMPILE** (unescaped `&` ×9; a single `\` where `\\` is needed at 16 table row-ends) → `pj_sub_buildcopy.pdf`, **7 pages**, built from the repaired copy and labelled as such. **Standing rule: never assign an execution step to an agent whose toolset lacks Bash** (`doc-curator`, `paper-writer`, `web-scout` in this rig; `paper-referee`, `results-analyst`, `experiment-engineer` have it).

**⭐ PART A — the Head's question, answered: the arithmetic is CLEAN.** V2: *"no number is fabricated and no number is mis-transcribed in any digit."* V5 the same on its numeric spine. **The condensation did not damage a single measured value.**

**⛔⛔ PART B — and this is why Part B existed: the CLAIMS drifted, in five specific ways, and two are serious.**
1. ⛔⛔ **V5 now AFFIRMS certified removal where the clean base DENIED it.** The base reads *"we do not claim certified (ε,δ) unlearning"*; `pj_sub.tex` states it affirmatively. **"Certified" for R1 deletion is a standing never-quote (charter §4; N118; CM-25(f)), and *certified removal* is a term of art (Guo §2 Eq. (1)) with a formal guarantee we do not have.** This is the most serious claims error the program has produced in this campaign, and it is one sentence.
2. ⛔ **V5 states store-level deletion without its three conditions and without the recency exclusion** — the one condition the program made permanent. The phrase *"explicit operational constraints"* names none of them.
3. ⛔ **V5's score sentence is absent**, and so is the deletion section's trivial-substitute laundering control.
4. ⛔ **Both papers lost their prior-art disclaimers.** V5 keeps the Blelloch–Golovin citation ×4 but drops *"we claim no priority… the fix-up cascade is theirs"*; V2 deleted the anchor's prior-art credit outright, so **the anchor now reads as ours**.
5. ⛔ **V2 carries four claim-strength widenings** (the constitutive identity; the anchored survival — the report's *"most quantitatively wrong sentence in the file"*; the anchor cure; and one more), each quoted against its source form.

**⭐ THE TWO BLIND PASSES AGREE — which is the whole point of barring the referees from the fidelity reports.** Both found the missing prior-art credit independently; both found V5's designed-only contrast reading as generalizing and the vault reading as transferring. **Agreement between an audit and an adversarial reader is the strongest signal available, and it is what makes these findings safe to act on.**

**⛔ TWO STRUCTURAL GAPS THE REFEREES FOUND THAT THE AUDIT COULD NOT.** V2's `pj_sub.tex` contains **zero `\cite` commands, no bibliography at all**, and **zero `\includegraphics`** — a paper claiming a two-branch law with a crossover and a floor, shipping **no figure**, while five rendered PNGs sit in its own `figs/`. It also asserts unattributed material belonging to a named neighbour. **V2's referee reads it as *a compressed full paper, not an extended abstract* — "the compression's signature failure."**

**⭐ V5's TRACK QUESTION, now answered with measurement (Add.52 live):** the referee measures **4.04 pp main / 7 pp total** — it *fits* the short track after a two-line trim — **and independently recommends the 9-pp FULL track anyway**, on the same reasoning the Advisor gave at Add.52 (the short-track ruling rested on a page number since proved typographic). **Two independent readings now agree: V5 → PALM full track.**

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-22.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 68 (2026-08-24) — the bibliography lands after three import rounds · ⭐ `FCluRefs_FIXED.bib` = 53 entries, full coverage, compile-verified clean · four silent defects caught that no tool warns about · ⚠ an Advisor instrument error, positive-controlled and corrected before it misled the Head

**The Head generated `refs.bib` from the Add.66 identifier block across three rounds; the Advisor checked each on disk.** Round 1 (`v2CLU_refs.bib`, 25 entries): **every DOI resolved, every arXiv-only work silently dropped** — 24 absent, including all three load-bearing citations (the head-to-head anchor, the CHLU self-cite, the Welling SSB paper). Round 2 (`v2CLU_refsN.bib`, 31): only 3 arXiv works added, **each imported twice**; 21 still absent. Round 3 (`FCluRefs.bib`, 46): the 21 landed, but the 3 duplicated ones dropped out and none of the identified defects were repaired. ⇒ **the identifiers were never at fault** — Advisor-verified all 21 against the arXiv API, every one resolving with the correct title. **The import tool was the failure surface, and it failed silently in a different way each round** — which is the case for verifying an exported bibliography against its source list every time rather than trusting a successful-looking export.

**⚠ ADVISOR INSTRUMENT ERROR, OWNED — and it is why the positive-control doctrine exists.** The Advisor's first arXiv check reported that **none** of the missing IDs resolved, which would have told the Head their identifier list was invalid. **A positive control on an ID known to have imported successfully failed too** ⇒ the instrument, not the data, was broken: the query used `http://export.arxiv.org`, which 301-redirects to nothing without `-L`. Re-run over HTTPS, **all 21 resolved**. ⛔ **No false claim reached the Head.** ⭐ Standing: *every negative result is positive-controlled before it is reported* — the rule held under exactly the conditions it was written for.

**⭐ DELIVERED: `~/Desktop/FCluRefs_FIXED.bib` — 53 entries (49 identifiers + 3 PMLR hand-entries + the Anonymous theory note), full coverage, no duplicate keys, and compile-verified** (`pdflatex → bibtex → pdflatex ×2` over a document citing every key: **0 BibTeX warnings · 0 LaTeX errors · 53/53 typeset · 0 undefined citations**). Four defects repaired that **no tool warns about**: ⛔ **the RBM-divergence paper was credited to the Springer LNCS *series editors* (Hutchison, Kanade, Kittler…) instead of Fischer & Igel** — the entry the cite-check flagged as owning the landscape-distortion result, so it would have mis-attributed a load-bearing citation in print · the Geometric Numerical Integration book had **no author field at all** · the GMOR title had been **flattened from Crossref MathML** to "SU 3 × SU 3" · and a **Unicode thin space in an author field would have failed the Head's build outright**. Also stripped: 31 Zotero `file = {…/Users/<name>/Zotero/…}` fields (a username riding inside a double-blind submission archive) and 13 abstracts — 78 KB → 28 KB. ⚠ Advisor-generated entries (the 21 + 3 arXiv works) are built **from the arXiv API directly**, bypassing the failing tool; every field traces to that record.

⚠ **Open, one word each:** the anchor's cite key is `mo_symmetryprotected_2026` — keys are source-only and never printed, and the bibliography legitimately names its author (Add.51 permits exactly that), but the Head may prefer a neutral key given how strictly the token is policed. And the ⛔ **numeric-citation-style requirement (Add.66 Ruling 1) still binds**: under an author–year style the three `\cite` sites render the author's name in body text and a section heading.

**Blind independence between passes** (referees barred from fidelity reports and from each other) produced convergent findings three times and is the single most trustworthy signal in the estate. **Scripted, assertion-guarded edits** (single-occurrence replacements, each asserting `count==1`) closed a rider restoration in one pass with an empty orphan list where a hand-rewrite had introduced four regressions. **The diff contract** (every hunk labelled, OTHER forced to zero) is how a "nothing but citations" promise becomes checkable. ⭐ **Numerical self-check on a derivation** — the theorist reproduced the paper's published constants from its own algebra (floor 27.0268 vs 27.03) — converted an unverifiable claim into a referee-proof one, and is the pattern for any future proof appendix.

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 91 (2026-08-26) — a SECOND, STRICTLY-BLIND reviewer scoped alongside the in-flight close-it pass · two instruments at different access levels on one artifact

**Head direction:** with `v5-referee-final` in flight, scope a second reviewer that is **purely a blind reviewer of the PDF**.

**Spoke scoped: `tasks/v5-blind-review.md` (paper-referee → `outputs/v5-blind-review.md`).** ⭐ **The design point is the ACCESS DIFFERENTIAL, and it is deliberate:**
- `v5-referee-final` is an **internal audit** — it may read `paper.tex`, `refs.bib` and the template, and it returns MUST/SHOULD/NICE with the cost of not fixing each, plus a submit/don't verdict.
- `v5-blind-review` is **the review we would actually receive** — ⛔ **it may open exactly one file, `paper.pdf`, and nothing else on the machine**: no `.tex`, no `.bib`, no build notes, nothing under `.claude/`. It returns an OpenReview-shaped review: summary, strengths, ranked weaknesses, questions, **unhedged recommendation + confidence + soundness/presentation scores**, venue fit, and the single strongest hostile objection with whether the paper answers it.
⇒ **Where two instruments at different access levels converge independently, the finding is trustworthy; where they diverge, the divergence is itself the signal** — typically that something is true of the source but invisible in the artifact. Both are barred from each other's reports, and the blind pass is told a sibling is running precisely so it does not go looking for it.

**⭐ Two instrument choices worth recording:**
1. **The blind reviewer must read the PDF *visually*, via page ranges, not as extracted text** — figure legibility at printed size is one of its questions and a text dump cannot answer it. This program has already shipped three figures roughly one inch tall; only a pass that *looks* at the page catches that class.
2. ⭐ **A required section: *"what I could not verify from the submission alone."*** This is the reviewer's real epistemic position — the exact gap between what the authors believe they have shown and what a reviewer can confirm from 18 pages. ⛔ And the task converts the urge to look something up into a **finding rather than a lookup**: *"if you catch yourself wanting to check something outside the PDF, that impulse is itself a finding."*

⛔ **Neither pass is told that the score sentence was removed by ruling** (Add.90.1(2)). If either independently reports that the paper never discloses what it fails to achieve, that is evidence the Head can weigh against their own decision; pre-empting it would destroy the only instrument capable of testing it.

**Parallel-safe:** both read-only on the same PDF, separate report files, zero writes to `paper.tex`, `refs.bib` or `NIPSsubmission/v5-palm/**`.

*Filed by the Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-26.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 92 (2026-08-26) — BOTH FINAL REVIEWS REVIEWED-ACCEPTED · ⛔⛔ ONE MUST IS REAL AND ADVISOR-CONFIRMED: the Limitations contradict a FIRED pre-registered falsifier · verdicts `SUBMIT AFTER THE MUSTS` (audit) and `borderline` (blind) · ⭐ four-way convergence on the trilemma and the 11-orders claim

**Verdicts — both ACCEPTED.** Artifacts byte-untouched by either pass (`paper.pdf` `e2cb5e09…`, `paper.tex`/`pj_sub.tex` `9e94731c…` before and after). **`v5-referee-final`: `SUBMIT AFTER THE MUSTS`** (6 MUSTs, each with a stated cost). **`v5-blind-review`: borderline** — soundness 3/4, presentation 2/4, confidence 4.

**Independence held, and the one departure was disclosed unprompted and well-handled.** The blind pass opened **only `paper.pdf`**, read as rendered pages so figures were judged as printed. The audit pass declared reading `critique_register.md` — dated 2026-07-20, five weeks older than this draft, containing no V5 assessment — **only after its blind main-text read was complete and written to `scratch/v5-referee-final/blind-first-read.md`** (Advisor-verified: that file exists and predates the report, 13:19 vs 13:29); it declined `claims_matrix.md` and `philosophy-synthesis.md` on the grounds they might carry V5-adjacent approved wordings. ⭐ *Declaring a judgment call rather than hiding it is the behaviour the bar exists to produce.*

### 92.1 ⛔⛔ THE ONE FINDING THE ADVISOR CONFIRMED FIRST-HAND, AND IT IS THE MOST SERIOUS
**The main text states the opposite of a pre-registered falsifier that the appendix says FIRED.** Verified verbatim on disk:
- **App. D:** *"the measured field-to-scalar control variance computes to `23.39±10.06` against a pre-registered bounds parameter of `[6.5,9.5]`, **triggering the designated falsifier constraint**…"*
- **§3 Limitations, Future Work bullet:** *"We intend to … remeasure the memory vault contrast arm **where bounds remain strictly contained**…"*
⇒ a main-text reader is told the bounds held; the appendix says they were exceeded by ~2.5×. ⭐ **BOTH passes found this independently** — the audit made it MUST-1 (*"the fastest available route from borderline to reject, and the only finding a rebuttal cannot repair — you cannot un-write a sentence a reviewer has read"*), and the blind reviewer, with no access beyond the PDF, raised it as a question to the authors (*"the emergent contrast-arm falsifier fired… why does the main-text vault claim not carry this caveat?"*). ⛔ Flagged, not fixed — the Head's prose.

### 92.2 ⭐ CONVERGENCES — where independent instruments agree, act first
1. ⭐⭐ **The "strict trilemma" is asserted, not established — THREE independent instruments now.** The audit (MUST-2: *"the single clearest instance of the designed-testbed result reading as general"*), the blind reviewer (W4), and — before either — **the physics theorist**, who listed it in its *could-not-derive* section because the paper states a formal impossibility with no proof. ⛔ Open and unruled since Add.87.
2. ⭐⭐ **The "11 orders of magnitude" claim is undercut by the paper's own fine print — FOUR-way.** The audit (abstract L14 + §2.1), the blind reviewer (W6: *"the low endpoint is the ring-profile probe's resolution"*), and the Advisor's own Add.87-amendment flag that **the abstract compresses away the instrument rider `pj_sub.tex` carries at l.95**. This is the same object the Head asked about at Add.87's adjudication; the papers are internally consistent, but the *abstract* is where the rider is missing.
3. **The privacy / membership-inference section** — audit MUST-3 (the `0.983` vs `1.000` sentence is genuinely ambiguous about which arm scores which) ↔ blind W5 (*"the adversary models are nonstandard"*). This is the section a PALM privacy reviewer reads twice.
4. **Figure 2** — blind calls the headline figure illegible; the audit finds panel (c) *"plots the number the paper disavows and not the number it quotes"*. Different defects, same figure.
5. **Program-internal vocabulary leaks into the artifact** — the audit's SF-6 (*"verification"/"evidence" used in main-text captions and defined nowhere*) is the C-2 taxonomy, and the blind reviewer independently complains of *"dense private jargon"*. ⭐ An instrument with no access to our conventions is the only one that can measure this.

### 92.3 ⚠ ONE DIVERGENCE, AND THE ADVISOR CORRECTS THE BLIND PASS
**Blind W3 — *"the vault headline rests on an estimator choice a reviewer cannot adjudicate"* — is OVERSTATED, and the Advisor's on-disk check is the authority.** `pj_sub.tex` l.97 (main text) carries **all three numbers adjacently**: the quoted `107.77±4.78×`, the uniform-scalar-friction baseline `13.28±0.12×`, and the raw first-passage `86.97±2.94×` with its boundary-layer bias and `ℓ_θ/Δ=0.079`, and it names the estimator explicitly. ⇒ **the data is not missing; the reviewer read it and still could not adjudicate**, which makes this a *presentation* finding rather than a disclosure failure. ⭐ The audit's sharper form is the actionable one (**SF-1**): **the abstract quotes `107.77×` naked, with the control main-text-only.**

### 92.4 ⛔ THE WITHHELD-ITEM TEST — RESULT: NEITHER PASS RAISED IT
Neither reviewer was told the score sentence and negatives appendix were removed by ruling (Add.90). **Neither flagged their absence.** The blind reviewer instead recorded the opposite as a *strength* — **S2, *"unusually honest scoping and adversarial self-testing"*** — citing the surviving Scope paragraph, §3, and the explicit *"no certified (ε,δ) unlearning"* denial. ⇒ **the Head's judgment at Add.90.1(2) is supported by the only instruments capable of testing it**, and the record should say so as plainly as it recorded the Advisor's objection. ⛔ Closed.

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 101 (2026-08-28) — the Head asks whether the economic register was fully retired · ⛔ ANSWER: NO — five sites were ESCALATED AND NEVER RULED, and one was a REGRESSION a later pass introduced · all six now fixed on Head instruction · ⭐ the ref-conversion gate is DISCHARGED ON THE FACTS

**Head: *"did we not remove all pricing/cost/pay terminology from v1?"*** ⛔ **No.** The `v1-terms-swap` pass (R3) did its job — `receipt` · `ledger` · `paid access` · `metered` · `priced` · `pricing` · `savings` all reached **0 and stayed there** — but its own §1 ruled *"a class not listed is out of scope"*, so it **stopped at the boundary and escalated the residue to the Head with drafted replacement wordings** (its open questions 2–3). ⚠ **That ruling was never given, so the sites simply sat.** ⭐ *The pass did not fail; the escalation did — an open question with no owner is indistinguishable from a finished job when you only read the summary line.*

### 101.2 ⛔ AND ONE WAS A REGRESSION — a completed cleanup has a shelf life too
**`buys` measured 0 in the post-swap snapshot and 1 in the live file.** Traced against banked bytes rather than argued: the **figure-insert pass (R4)** added the `fig:bibo` caption containing *"so what **buys** boundedness is the screen"* — **after** the vocabulary pass had cleared the word. ⚠ **This is Add.97 §97.6(1) recurring in a new costume** (there, a figure spoke re-rendered *"priced out"* into the headline figure). ⇒ **STANDING RULE, earned twice: any pass that writes NEW PROSE — captions especially — must carry the retired-vocabulary list, or it will quietly re-open a closed cleanup.** ⛔ The swap's own verification was correct when written; it decayed.

### 101.3 Executed on Head instruction (*"apply fixes without changing the rest"*)
Six edits, each `count==1`-asserted, backed up first (`scratch/v1-cfix/pj_sub.PRE-register.tex`), **6 changed lines in 5 hunks and nothing else**:

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | contribution 2 | `prices reach exponentially in rapidity` → `extends reach at an energy exponential in the rapidity` | ⚠ **ADVISOR, departing from the draft — disclosed** |
| 2 | §2 | `energy cannot unilaterally buy flow reach` → `…unilaterally extend flow reach` | Advisor (no draft existed) |
| 3 | §3.1 | `unpaid contraction` → `uncompensated contraction` | drafted by the swap pass |
| 4 | §3.2.1 heading | `Cashing Out the Certificate:` → `The Certificate's Consequence:` | drafted |
| 5 | **Fig-1 caption (b)** | `The certificate cashed out:` → `The certificate in force:` | drafted |
| 6 | **`fig:bibo` caption** | `what buys boundedness` → `what provides boundedness` | Advisor (the §101.2 regression) |

**Verified in the RENDERED PDF, not the source:** all six new forms print; `Cashing Out` · `cashed out` · `buys` · `unpaid` · `prices reach` · `payoff` = **0 in the PDF text**; build **0 errors · 0 undefined citations · 0 undefined references · 0 literal `??` · 15 pp**. ## ⟲ ADDENDUM 102 (2026-08-29) — the gated-compute-allocation figure BANKED in two variants per Head ruling · the re-render is validated against the paper's own printed numbers · ⚠⚠ AND THE FIGURE SHOWS A NAIVE BASELINE ABOVE THE LEARNED GATE AT THE KNEE — a new finding, surfaced by plotting it

### 102.1 What was asked and what landed
**V1 expert request + Head rulings (2026-08-29): (1) produce the figure BOTH with and without the Hopfield reference line — the Head decides visually; (2) placement (join vs replace `fig_frontier_clean`) also decided visually.** ⇒ **Both variants are banked, additively, in `NIPSsubmission/v1-ttcl/figs/`:**
- **`fig_compute_allocation.png`** — ⭐ **the ORIGINAL July artifact, byte-identical** (`fcab7098…`, from `outputs/v1-pivot/full/plots/exp_v1_calibration_compute.png`). **This is what ships if the Head keeps Hopfield** — no re-render risk on the shipping path.
- **`fig_compute_allocation_nohopfield.png`** — the re-render with the Hopfield `axhline` removed (`06c65e99…`).
- For a strict like-for-like A/B (same renderer, ⛔ only the red line differing) `scratch/v1-compute-fig/REPRO_with_hopfield.png` is the companion to the no-Hopfield file.

### 102.2 ⛔ HOW THE RE-RENDER WAS MADE SAFE — the saved artifacts did NOT contain the curves
⚠ **The plot could not simply be replotted:** `results/` holds the per-query raw arrays (`R`, `margin`, `correct`, `cost`, `pw_*`) and scalar summaries, **but not the swept accuracy-vs-cost curves** — those had to be recomputed. A recomputation that silently moves a plotted value is exactly what the `tap.py` discipline exists to prevent, so:
1. ⭐ **`_simulate_tau_policy` was COPIED VERBATIM** from `chlu/experiments/exp_v1_gate.py:321`, and the panel construction verbatim from `exp_v1_calibration.py:878–928` — ⛔ **copied, never reimplemented**; no JAX imported (the plotting path is pure NumPy/matplotlib).
2. **`pw_deployed` resolved from the data, not assumed:** `summary["gate_features"] == "r_margin"` ⇒ `pw_rm`.
3. ⭐⭐ **VALIDATION 1 — the operating points reproduce the paper EXACTLY**, and they come from the summary JSON independently of the recomputed curves: **acc 0.8938 @ 629 steps, savings 4.81× / 1.57× / 1.14×** against §4.1's printed `4.81±0.44×`, `1.57±0.07×`, `1.14±0.06×`.
4. ⭐ **VALIDATION 2 — the recomputed curve passes through that independently-known point**: Δaccuracy **0.0039 / 0.0030 / 0.0005** across kv16/24/32 (residual expected: the point is one τ, the curve is interpolated onto a 24-point cost grid).
5. ⚠ **One input is NOT recorded anywhere and is declared, not hidden:** `calib_n_policy_taus` (default **25**; the run log records config but never this field — positive-controlled). **Sensitivity measured rather than argued: N_TAUS ∈ {15,20,30,40} moves accuracy by ≤ 0.007 and does not change the shape.**
6. ⚠ **Pixel-identity was NOT achieved and is not claimed:** the re-render is 593×2231 against the original's 595×2229 — matplotlib/font drift since July. ⛔ **This is why the ORIGINAL, not the re-render, is banked as the with-Hopfield variant.**

### 102.3 ⚠⚠ NEW FINDING — THE FIGURE DISPLAYS A NAIVE BASELINE ABOVE THE LEARNED GATE
**Measured, not eyeballed, on the curves being shipped:**
| panel | learned gate (swept) peak | margin-gated (swept) peak | raw-R peak |
|---|---|---|---|
| **kv16** | 0.8988 @ 652 | ⛔ **0.9168 @ 652** (±0.0233) | 0.8720 @ 1591 |
| kv24 | 0.5513 @ 1474 | 0.5575 @ 1709 | 0.5588 @ 1474 |
| kv32 | 0.2978 @ 1591 | 0.3052 @ 1943 | 0.2933 @ 1591 |
⇒ **At the kv16 knee — the paper's headline cell — the naive margin gate sits ~1.8 points ABOVE the calibrated learned gate at the same compute**, and margin/raw-R edge it out at kv24/kv32 too (all well inside the ±0.02–0.07 seed spread). ⭐ **This is not a contradiction of §4.1, which already concedes the gate's asset is compute rationing and ⛔ explicitly disclaims "inherently superior energy signal" — but the current text states that in prose while this figure states it in a picture a referee reads first.** ⛔ **Head decision, not the Advisor's:** ship as-is (the honest reading, and consistent with the §97.2.2 no-comparative-claim framing), or let the caption name it before a referee does. ⚠ **Note the interaction with the Hopfield ruling: removing the red line does NOT remove the "we are beaten on this plot" reading — the margin-gated curve carries it too, and it is one of our own arms.**

### 102.4 Caption fences that travel with EITHER variant (from the expert, Advisor-verified as still binding)
⛔ **CM-23(b) is shape-only; the absolute-dominance reading is RETRACTED (N90); N95 confirms the loss survives with headroom deliberately built in ⇒ saturation is NOT available as an explanation.** ⛔ The claim form is **"same accuracy at ~1/10th the steps," NEVER "more compute buys more accuracy"** (V1's own converged grid has gate accuracy *equal to* full-budget at 2000 ep). ⚠ **The kv16 panel is honestly non-monotonic** — it rises to the knee and then declines back to always-full; that is visible in the artifact and must not be described as a monotone gain. ⭐ The figure also **restores the step counts** (629/1919/2636 vs 3000) that the current text dropped in favour of the ratios alone.

*Filed by the V1 Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-29.*

## ⟲ ADDENDUM 103 (2026-08-29) — `v1-compute-curve-converged` REVIEWED-ACCEPTED · ⭐⭐ the naive-arm inversion is GONE at convergence · ⛔⛔ but the converged curve is FLAT — the figure proves more and shows less · ⛔ AND THE PAPER'S EPOCH NUMBER IS WRONG AT FOUR SITES

### 103.1 Verdict — ACCEPTED
`outputs/v1-compute-curve-converged.md`. **13.7 min wall for 15 cell-seeds** (budgeted 1–3 h). ⛔ **Scope clean, Advisor-verified independently:** `figs/` untouched (`06c65e99…` unmoved) · the Advisor's own `scratch/v1-compute-fig/` renders unmodified (the spoke copied rather than mutated) · ⚠ `pj_sub.tex` did move, but at **10:13**, hours before the spoke ran — **the Head's own edit, not a violation.**
**Acceptance criteria met:** all three panels ≥ 0.8 (**0.9969 / 0.9938 / 0.9052**) · the learned operating points reproduce the banked regime numbers with **every deviation < 1σ** (savings 9.74 vs 9.89 · 9.74 vs 9.54 · 5.54 vs 6.09) · the pre-flight check re-verified the re-run was necessary (0 list-valued keys across all 198 banked cells) · ⭐ **the epoch clamp was verified NOT in force** rather than assumed (`min(train_epochs,120)` sits inside `if quick:`). ⚠ **One confound the spoke named itself and the Advisor endorses:** the regime and calibration harnesses differ (`_clustered_embeddings` vs `make_token_embeddings`; different episode counts), so this is *two harnesses at the same configuration*, ⛔ not two runs of one configuration — the agreement is corroboration, not replication.

### 103.2 ⭐⭐ THE §4 QUESTION IS ANSWERED — the inversion does not survive training
At **500 ep** the naive margin arm beat the learned gate (kv16 knee **0.9168 vs 0.8988**; ahead on 14/24 matched-compute points). **At 2000 ep it never wins:** at (128,32) and (256,64) all three arms are **bit-identical (24/24 tied)**; at (384,96) learned and margin reach the **same peak 0.90521** but the learned gate arrives at **417 steps vs 652 — a 1.56× cheaper knee** — and strictly dominates raw-R. ⇒ **the Add.102 §102.3 finding is REGIME-BOUND, not a property of the gate**, and the converged figure carries no "beaten by our own naive arm" reading. ⭐ *The measurement the Head asked for retired a live risk to the paper.*

### 103.3 ⛔⛔ THE COST OF THAT WIN — at convergence there is no curve to draw
Straight from the arrays: at (128,32) and (256,64) **not one query out of 128, in any of 5 seeds, changes its answer between 300 and 3000 Verlet steps** (stage-wise accuracy identical to 5 decimals); at (384,96) ≤ 3.1 % of queries change and the swing is ≤ 0.004 — **under one query in 192, i.e. noise, and non-monotonic**. ⇒ **The read is fully settled at stage 0.** This is simultaneously **the strongest possible form of the sanctioned claim — ~10× fewer steps at literally identical accuracy** — and the reason the panels are **flat lines**: the default render autoscales panel 1 onto `0.993–1.001`, which reads as noise. A **shared-y variant** (`…_sharedy.png`, common `0.86–1.02` axis) is the honest render and the one to use if a converged figure ships.
⚠ **The trade, stated plainly for the Head, and it is a real one:** the 500-ep figure *shows* a knee but at accuracies 0.85/0.53/0.28 with a naive arm ahead; the 2000-ep figure *proves* the claim at 0.99/0.99/0.91 with the gate never losing, but a reviewer sees three flat lines and may read "the compute dial does nothing." ⛔ **Advisor recommendation is NOT a figure choice — it is that the claim at these cells is a NUMBER, not a curve**, and the caption must say what the flatness means (the ladder is unnecessary once the memory is trained) or the figure argues against itself.

### 103.4 ⛔ RECONCILIATION LIST — the paper's epoch budget is wrong, and it sits on the headline number
⭐ **The spoke's premise check caught an error the Advisor's own task file had propagated.** Ground truth, both polarities: `outputs/v1-pivot/full/run_log.txt` reads *"Training generative model for **500** epochs"* **90/90 times, with 0 hits for 400**; `config.py:358` default `train_epochs = 500`.
⛔ **WRONG at FOUR sites, every one describing the calibration run that produces `4.81±0.44×`:** **l.116** (*"under-converged 400-epoch training budget"*) · **l.146** (*"at a 400-epoch budget"*) · **l.241** (*"The calibrated gate evaluation utilizes γ=0.3 and 400 epochs"*) · **l.187**, the **A.2 Calibrated Gate flag-provenance table** row `epochs 400`. ⚠ **The measured numbers are unaffected — only the label is wrong** — but this is a flag-provenance defect on the headline claim's configuration, the exact class the M4 rule exists to prevent, and it is *in the provenance table itself*.
✅ **NOT wrong, and checked rather than assumed: l.202's `epochs 400` in the ROUTING table is CORRECT** — the router run's own log reads *"for 400 epochs"* 60/60. ⭐ **A blanket "five sites say 400, fix them all" would have corrupted a correct number**; the tables cover different experiments.
**Two further items owed an owner:** §4.1's **`6.2×`** for (384,96) comes from the **regime** harness (this harness gives `5.54 ± 0.20` vs banked `6.09 ± 0.85`, 0.64σ — not a conflict, but the provenance should be named); and any caption asserting a visible accuracy-vs-compute **curve** must change if the converged figure ships.

*Filed by the V1 Shorts Advisor, 2026-08-29.*
